<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove that approximating Max-Cut on simple unweighted graphs within any fixed factor greater than the Goemans–Williamson constant is NP-hard.

- **stmt-thm-main** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): For every fixed $\alpha$ with $\alpha_{\mathrm{GW}}<\alpha\le1$, it is NP-hard to approximate Max-Cut within a factor of $\alpha$. This holds for simple unweighted graphs.
  - Explanation: The claimed hardness threshold applies to every fixed approximation factor above the Goemans–Williamson constant on simple unweighted graphs.
- **stmt-thm-gap** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-gap)): Fix a rational $t\in(0,1)$ and a constant $0<\varepsilon<(t-B(t))/4$. For graphs equipped with a rational probability distribution on edges, it is NP-hard to distinguish $$\begin{aligned} \textnormal{YES:}\quad&\mathop{\mathrm{Val}}\ge\frac{1+t}{2}-\varepsilon,\\ \textnormal{NO:}\quad&\mathop{\mathrm{Val}}\le\frac{1+B(t)}{2}+\varepsilon, \end{aligned}$$ where $\mathop{\mathrm{Val}}$ is the maximum cut weight.
  - Explanation: The rational edge-distribution promise separates the stated completeness and soundness cut values.
- **stmt-lem-mis** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-mis)): Fix $0<t<1$ and $\xi>0$. There are an integer $K\ge1$ and $\tau>0$, depending only on $t,\xi$, such that every $h:\{-1,1\}^\Omega\to[-1,1]$ with $\mathbb Eh=0$ satisfies $$\bigl(\mathop{\mathrm{Inf}}_a^{\le K}(h)<\tau\text{ for all }a\in\Omega\bigr) \quad\Longrightarrow\quad \mathop{\mathrm{Stab}}_t(h)\le B(t)+\xi.$$
  - Explanation: Balanced bounded functions with sufficiently small degree-truncated influences have noise stability bounded by the Gaussian curve and the allowed error.
- **stmt-prop-affine-games** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-affine-games)): For every constant $b\in(0,1)$ there is an integer $n\geq 1$ such that, for every constant $a\in(0,1-b)$, the following promise problem is NP-hard. Its input is a weighted bipartite game with vertex sets $X,Y$, respective label sets $\mathbb F_2^{n+1},\mathbb F_2^n$, and a surjective affine map $$\pi_e:\mathbb F_2^{n+1}\longrightarrow\mathbb F_2^n$$ on each edge $e=(x,y)$. An edge is satisfied when $\pi_e(\lambda_X(x))=\lambda_Y(y)$. The promise cases are $$\mathrm{YES}:\quad \mathop{\mathrm{Val}}(\mathcal G)\geq 1-a, \qquad \mathrm{NO}:\quad \mathop{\mathrm{Val}}(\mathcal G)\leq b.$$ The integer $n$ depends on $b$ alone. For fixed $a,b$, the reduction is deterministic and has polynomial running time and output size.
  - Explanation: The source-game alphabet is fixed by soundness before the completeness error is chosen; constraints are surjective affine projections.
- **stmt-lem-tree-correlation** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-tree-correlation)): For every fixed collection of tables $F$, the root output as a multilinear polynomial in the $k$ leaf signs has only degree-$d$ monomials. Each such monomial chooses one child from every pair at every node that it reaches. Every resulting traversal meets every possible slice $\mathcal D$ at exactly one node. Consequently, negating all the tables on a slice negates $W$.* *Let $0<t<1$ and $0<r<1$. Draw $F$ uniformly, and construct $F'$ by leaving the tables outside $\mathcal D$ unchanged and coupling each pair of corresponding table entries on $\mathcal D$ as uniform signs of correlation $t$, independently across entries. Independently draw $R\in H^k$ uniformly, and form $R^1,R^2$ by retaining each row of $R$ with probability $r$ and otherwise replacing it by an independent uniform row, independently in the two copies. Then, for every $a\in\mathcal A$ and every fixed slice, $$\mathbb E\bigl[W(R^1,F)(a)W(R^2,F')(a)\mid\mathcal D\bigr] =t r^{2d}. \label{eq:tree-correlation}$$ In particular, if $r=1-\mu/d$ with $0<\mu<1/2$, then $r^{2d}\ge1-2\mu$.
  - Explanation: Complementary traversals force homogeneous leaf degree and a single correlated slice contribution to dictator correlation.
- **stmt-lem-completeness** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-completeness)): If $\mathop{\mathrm{Val}}(\mathcal G)\ge1-a$, the graph has a cut of value at least $$\label{eq:completeness} (1-2Ta)\frac{1+t r^{2d}}2 \ge\frac{1+t}{2}-t\mu-2Ta.$$ The first lower bound is useful when $2Ta\le1$, which is the regime used below.
  - Explanation: Satisfied source labels produce a dictator cut, with losses from row resampling and unsatisfied sampled constraints.
- **stmt-lem-cut-stability** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-cut-stability)): For every cut of the constructed graph, $$\label{eq:cut-stability} \operatorname{cut}(\sigma)=\frac12+ \frac12\mathbb E\mathop{\mathrm{Stab}}_t^{F_{\mathcal D}}(\bar G_{\underline y}) -\frac12\mathbb E\mathop{\mathrm{Stab}}_t^{F_{\mathcal D}}(\bar A_{\underline y}),$$ where both expectations are over $\underline y,\mathcal D,R,F_{\mathrm{out}}$. In particular, $$\label{eq:cut-upper-stability} \operatorname{cut}(\sigma)\le\frac12+ \frac12\mathbb E\mathop{\mathrm{Stab}}_t^{F_{\mathcal D}}(\bar G_{\underline y}).$$
  - Explanation: Odd dependence increases the cut value while even dependence subtracts a nonnegative stability contribution.
- **stmt-lem-row-symmetry** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-row-symmetry)): Let $f$ be a function of the code. Every nonzero row Fourier coefficient of $f$ satisfies, at each internal node $v$, $$q_{v,g0}+q_{v,g1}=q_v\qquad(g\in[s]). \label{eq:row-relations}$$ For a table collection $F$, let $F^{v,-}$ negate $F_v$ alone, and let $F^{v,z}$ replace $F_v(u)$ by $F_v(u+z)$, leaving all other tables unchanged. Then $$\begin{aligned} \widehat f_C(F^{v,-}) &=(-1)^{q_v(\mathbf 1)}\widehat f_C(F), \label{eq:table-flip-action}\\ \widehat f_C(F^{v,z}) &=(-1)^{\sum_g z_g q_{v,g1}(\mathbf 1)}\widehat f_C(F). \label{eq:table-translation-action} \end{aligned}$$ All these statements remain true after row smoothing. They are also preserved by row Fourier projections and by projection to any range of total Fourier degrees in the entries of a specified collection of whole gate tables.
  - Explanation: Word-preserving row transformations impose common pair sums and determine the signs induced by table actions.
- **stmt-lem-unsplit** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-unsplit)): Let $f$ be a function of the code with $\|f\|\le1$, let $r=1-\mu/d$ with $0<\mu<1/2$, and let $\bar f=T_rf$. Choose the random slice independently of $f$. Then $$\mathbb E_{J_0,\mathcal D}\sum_{v\in\mathcal D} \norm{(I-S_v)\bar f}_{2,R,F}^{2} \le \frac{s}{2e m\mu}. \label{eq:unsplit-bound}$$ The same bound holds after averaging over additional background data determining $f$, provided the slice is chosen independently of those data.
  - Explanation: At a random slice, row smoothing bounds the averaged cost of retaining unsplit Fourier indices.
- **stmt-lem-shift-coordinates** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-shift-coordinates)): The change of coordinates $$H^k\longrightarrow H^{2s}\times H^{k-2s},\qquad R\longmapsto((L_{g,b}),\Gamma)$$ is a linear bijection. In particular, under uniform rows the free shifts are independent uniforms conditional on $\Gamma$. The Fourier index of $L_{g,b}$ corresponding to a full row index $C$ is $Q_{g,b}=q_{v,g b}$.* *Consequently, at every fixed $\Gamma$, the operator $S_v$ acts as the Fourier projection in the $2s$ shifts onto precisely the unsplit shift indices. For a function of the code or its row smoothing, the shift coefficients vanish unless the sums $Q_{g,0}+Q_{g,1}$ are the same for every $g$. The table actions in Equations <a href="#eq:table-flip-action" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:table-flip-action">[eq:table-flip-action]</a> and <a href="#eq:table-translation-action" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:table-translation-action">[eq:table-translation-action]</a> hold for each shift coefficient at this fixed context, with $q_v=Q_{1,0}+Q_{1,1}$ and $q_{v,g1}=Q_{g,1}$.
  - Explanation: The coordinate bijection isolates independent free rows and makes the unsplit projection act separately at fixed contexts.
- **stmt-lem-hashing** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-hashing)): Let $H$ be a finite-dimensional vector space over $\mathbb F_2$, write $\chi_q(u)=(-1)^{q(u)}$ for $q\in H^*$ and $u\in H$, let $\mathcal V$ be a real Hilbert space, let $s\ge1$ be an integer, and let $$c(q,z)\in\mathcal V, \qquad q\in H^*,\quad z\in\mathbb F_2^s.$$ Set $$w_q=\sum_{z\in\mathbb F_2^s}\norm{c(q,z)}^2, \qquad M=\sum_{q\in H^*}w_q.$$ Choose $L,J_1,\ldots,J_s$ independently and uniformly in $H$, and define $$\mathbf J(q)=(q(J_1),\ldots,q(J_s)), \qquad U(z)=\sum_{q\in H^*}\chi_q(L)c(q,z+\mathbf J(q)).$$ Then $$\label{eq:hashing-total-mass} \mathbb E\sum_{z\in\mathbb F_2^s}\norm{U(z)}^2=M.$$ For every $\zeta>0$, there are $\gamma>0$ and $s_0\in\mathbb N$, depending only on $\zeta$, such that for every $s\ge s_0$, $$\label{eq:hashing-dispersion} \max_{q\in H^*}w_q\le\gamma M \quad\Longrightarrow\quad \mathbb E\max_{z\in\mathbb F_2^s}\norm{U(z)}^2\le\zeta M.$$
  - Explanation: Character orthogonality preserves total mass, while diffuse character weights bound the expected largest hashed vector.
- **stmt-prop-extraction** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-extraction)): Fix $t\in(0,1)$, $0<\delta<1-B(t)$, and $0<\mu<1/2$. There are tree parameters $s\ge2,m\ge1$ and constants $p_*,\theta>0$, depending only on $t,\delta,\mu$, with the following property. For any affine label space $\mathcal A$ and any family $\phi_\omega$ as above, if $$\label{eq:excess-stability} \mathbb E_{\omega,\mathcal D,R,F_{\mathrm{out}}} \mathop{\mathrm{Stab}}_t^{F_{\mathcal D}}(\bar f_\omega) \ge B(t)+\delta,$$ then $$\label{eq:extraction-event} \Pr\left[ \max_{q\in\mathcal A} m_q(\bar f_\omega;\Gamma,F_{\mathrm{out}})\ge\theta \right]\ge p_*.$$ Here we sample $\omega$, the random slice $\mathcal D$, and a uniform node $v\in\mathcal D$; conditional on these, we sample $F_{\mathrm{out}}$ and $\Gamma$ independently and uniformly.
  - Explanation: Excess slice stability forces a label with substantial conditional shift mass with constants independent of label-space dimension.
- **stmt-lem-unsmooth** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-unsmooth)): For every fixed $\underline y$, $\mathcal D$, $v$, $\Gamma$, $F_{\mathrm{out}}$, and label $q\in\mathcal A$, $$\label{eq:unsmooth} m_q(\bar G_{\underline y};\Gamma,F_{\mathrm{out}}) \leq \mathbb E_{E,\,\underline e\mid\underline y} m_q(G_{\underline e};\Gamma+E_\Gamma,F_{\mathrm{out}}).$$ Here the noise $E$ is independent of the conditionally sampled edge tuple $\underline e$.
  - Explanation: Convexity of squared projection norm bounds smoothed label mass by the expected raw mass after noise and edge sampling.
- **stmt-lem-planting** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-planting)): Let $\mathsf U$ be the unplanted law and $\mathsf P$ the planted law after forgetting the mark. Both laws are taken on the full data $$\bigl(\underline e,J_0,\mathcal D,v,F_{\mathrm{out}}, \Gamma,R^{\mathrm{new}},B\bigr).$$ Then $$\label{eq:planting-tv} \mathop{\mathrm{TV}}(\mathsf P,\mathsf U) \leq \frac12\sqrt{\frac{1-p_0}{Tp_0}} \leq \frac{1}{2\sqrt{Tp_0}}.$$ Consequently, if $$\label{eq:decoder-repetition} T\geq\frac{1}{p_0(p_*\theta)^2},$$ then under the planted law, $$\label{eq:planted-score} \mathbb E_{\mathsf P}\,m_{q^*}(G_{\underline e}; \Gamma+E_\Gamma,F_{\mathrm{out}}) \geq \frac{p_*\theta}{2}.$$
  - Explanation: Forgetting a uniformly planted zero coordinate controls total variation and retains a positive expected decoding score.
- **stmt-lem-restriction** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-restriction)): Fix a context and the outside tables. Let $\widetilde f(\widetilde L,F_{\mathcal D})$ be a function on $\widetilde H^{2s}$ and the slice tables. Suppose its shift Fourier support has the common-pair-sum property $$\widetilde Q_{g0}+\widetilde Q_{g1} =\widetilde Q_{h0}+\widetilde Q_{h1} \quad\text{for all }g,h\in[s].$$ Set $f(L,F_{\mathcal D})= \widetilde f(\iota L,F_{\mathcal D})$, where $\iota$ acts on each shift. For every label $q\in\mathcal A$, $$\label{eq:restriction-mass} m_q(f)\leq 2^{2s} \sum_{\substack{\widetilde q\in\widetilde{\mathcal A}\\ \rho(\widetilde q)=q}} \widetilde m_{\widetilde q}(\widetilde f).$$ The masses on both sides use the common-pair-sum definition of Equation <a href="#eq:mass" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:mass">[eq:mass]</a>, with the respective affine row spaces.
  - Explanation: Common-pair-sum Fourier support bounds the loss of label mass under an affine map with two-element fibers.
- **stmt-prop-decoding** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-decoding)): Suppose the extraction conclusion of Proposition <a href="#prop:extraction" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:extraction">13</a> holds with constants $p_*,\theta>0$. If $T$ satisfies Equation <a href="#eq:decoder-repetition" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:decoder-repetition">[eq:decoder-repetition]</a>, then the original affine game has value at least $$\label{eq:decoded-value} \mathop{\mathrm{Val}}(\mathcal G)\geq 2^{-2s}\frac{p_*\theta}{2}.$$
  - Explanation: Public randomness and hidden-coordinate planting produce endpoint labelers with the stated alphabet-independent success bound.
- **stmt-lem-affine-validity-invariance** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-affine-validity-invariance)): Let $X$ have dimension $3k_0$, let $H\le X$ have dimension $k_0$, and let $h:H\to\mathbb F_2$ be linear. Suppose a table $F$ assigns a linear function to every $\ell$-space, and that for every $(\ell+k_0)$-space $K\supseteq H$ there is a linear function $f_K:K\to\mathbb F_2$ such that $f_K\|_H=h$ and $F[L]=f_K\|_L$ whenever $L+H=K$. Fix a $q$-space $Q$ with $Q\cap H=\{0\}$, and set $$e_{k_0}=2^{\ell+r-2k_0+1}.$$ Assume $q\le\ell$, $3k_0-r>\ell$, <a href="#eq:affine-validity-ell-margin" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:affine-validity-ell-margin">[eq:affine-validity-ell-margin]</a>, and $e_{k_0}\le\tau_0/4$. For $Q\subseteq W\subseteq X$ of codimension $i\le r$, say that $(g,W)$ occurs if $g:W\to\mathbb F_2$ is linear and $$\Pr_{Q\subseteq L\subseteq W,\ \dim L=\ell} [F[L]=g\|_L]>\tau_i.$$ Call it maximal if it has no occurring extension to a strictly larger space. Every maximal occurring pair satisfies $H\subseteq W$ and $g\|_H=h$.
  - Explanation: Maximal occurring list pairs on admissible advice spaces contain the equation subspace and respect its functional.
- **stmt-lem-unweighted** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-unweighted)): Let $G$ be a graph on $N$ vertices with nonnegative rational weights $w_{uv}$ on unordered distinct vertex pairs, and $\sum_{u<v}w_{uv}\le1$. For every fixed $\epsilon'>0$, one can construct in deterministic polynomial time a simple unweighted graph $G'$ and an integer scale $Q$ such that $$\left\|\frac{\operatorname{MaxCut}(G')}{Q} -\operatorname{MaxCut}(G)\right\|\le\epsilon'.$$ The same conclusion holds when the input graph has loops, after deleting them without renormalizing its other weights.
  - Explanation: A deterministic cluster construction preserves maximum cut on a common scale with fixed additive error.

Draw $F$ uniformly, and construct $F'$ by leaving the tables outside $\\mathcal D$ unchanged and coupling each pair of corresponding table entries on $\\mathcal D$ as uniform signs of correlation $t$, independently across entries. Independently draw $R\\in H^k$ uniformly, and form $R^1,R^2$ by retaining each row of $R$ with probability $r$ and otherwise replacing it by an independent uniform row, independently in the two copies. Then, for every $a\\in\\mathcal A$ and every fixed slice, $$\\mathbb E\\bigl[W(R^1,F)(a)W(R^2,F')(a)\\mid\\mathcal D\\bigr]\n       =t r^{2d}.\n \\label{eq:tree-correlation}$$ In particular, if $r=1-\\mu/d$ with $0<\\mu<1/2$, then $r^{2d}\\ge1-2\\mu$.",
      "explanation": "Complementary traversals force homogeneous leaf degree and a single correlated slice contribution to dictator correlation.",
      "fidelity": "PENDING",
      "hypotheses": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-tree-correlation",
          "exact_quote": "For every fixed collection of tables $F$, the root output as a multilinear polynomial in the $k$ leaf signs has only degree-$d$ monomials.",
          "source_id": "PM-102.2"
        }
      ],

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-row-symmetry",
        "exact_quote": "Let $f$ be a function of the code. Every nonzero row Fourier coefficient of $f$ satisfies, at each internal node $v$, $$q_{v,g0}+q_{v,g1}=q_v\\qquad(g\\in[s]).\n \\label{eq:row-relations}$$ For a table collection $F$, let $F^{v,-}$ negate $F_v$ alone, and let $F^{v,z}$ replace $F_v(u)$ by $F_v(u+z)$, leaving all other tables unchanged. Then $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\widehat f_C(F^{v,-})\n   &=(-1)^{q_v(\\mathbf 1)}\\widehat f_C(F),\n   \\label{eq:table-flip-action}\\\\\n \\widehat f_C(F^{v,z})\n   &=(-1)^{\\sum_g z_g q_{v,g1}(\\mathbf 1)}\\widehat f_C(F).\n   \\label{eq:table-translation-action}\n\\end{aligned}$$ All these statements remain true after row smoothing. They are also preserved by row Fourier projections and by projection to any range of total Fourier degrees in the entries of a specified collection of whole gate tables.",
        "source_id": "PM-102.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-row-symmetry",
          "exact_quote": "Let $f$ be a function of the code. Then $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\widehat f_C(F^{v,-})\n   &=(-1)^{q_v(\\mathbf 1)}\\widehat f_C(F),\n   \\label{eq:table-flip-action}\\\\\n \\widehat f_C(F^{v,z})\n   &=(-1)^{\\sum_g z_g q_{v,g1}(\\mathbf 1)}\\widehat f_C(F).\n   \\label{eq:table-translation-action}\n\\end{aligned}$$ All these statements remain true after row smoothing.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-shift-coordinates",
        "exact_quote": "The change of coordinates $$H^k\\longrightarrow H^{2s}\\times H^{k-2s},\\qquad\n R\\longmapsto((L_{g,b}),\\Gamma)$$ is a linear bijection. In particular, under uniform rows the free shifts are independent uniforms conditional on $\\Gamma$. The Fourier index of $L_{g,b}$ corresponding to a full row index $C$ is $Q_{g,b}=q_{v,g b}$.*\n\n*Consequently, at every fixed $\\Gamma$, the operator $S_v$ acts as the Fourier projection in the $2s$ shifts onto precisely the unsplit shift indices. For a function of the code or its row smoothing, the shift coefficients vanish unless the sums $Q_{g,0}+Q_{g,1}$ are the same for every $g$. The table actions in Equations <a href=\"#eq:table-flip-action\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:table-flip-action\">[eq:table-flip-action]</a> and <a href=\"#eq:table-translation-action\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:table-translation-action\">[eq:table-translation-action]</a> hold for each shift coefficient at this fixed context, with $q_v=Q_{1,0}+Q_{1,1}$ and $q_{v,g1}=Q_{g,1}$.",
        "source_id": "PM-102.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-shift-coordinates",
          "exact_quote": "The change of coordinates $$H^k\\longrightarrow H^{2s}\\times H^{k-2s},\\qquad\n R\\longmapsto((L_{g,b}),\\Gamma)$$ is a linear bijection. In particular, under uniform rows the free shifts are independent uniforms conditional on $\\Gamma$. The Fourier index of $L_{g,b}$ corresponding to a full row index $C$ is $Q_{g,b}=q_{v,g b}$.*\n\n*Consequently, at every fixed $\\Gamma$, the operator $S_v$ acts as the Fourier projection in the $2s$ shifts onto precisely the unsplit shift indices. For a function of the code or its row smoothing, the shift coefficients vanish unless the sums $Q_{g,0}+Q_{g,1}$ are the same for every $g$. The table actions in Equations <a href=\"#eq:table-flip-action\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:table-flip-action\">[eq:table-flip-action]</a> and <a href=\"#eq:table-translation-action\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:table-translation-action\">[eq:table-translation-action]</a> hold for each shift coefficient at this fixed context, with $q_v=Q_{1,0}+Q_{1,1}$ and $q_{v,g1}=Q_{g,1}$.",
          "source_id": "PM-102.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "The change of coordinates $$H^k\\longrightarrow H^{2s}\\times H^{k-2s},\\qquad\n R\\longmapsto((L_{g,b}),\\Gamma)$$ is a linear bijection. In particular, under uniform rows the free shifts are independent uniforms conditional on $\\Gamma$. The Fourier index of $L_{g,b}$ corresponding to a full row index $C$ is $Q_{g,b}=q_{v,g b}$.*\n\n*Consequently, at every fixed $\\Gamma$, the operator $S_v$ acts as the Fourier projection in the $2s$ shifts onto precisely the unsplit shift indices. For a function of the code or its row smoothing, the shift coefficients vanish unless the sums $Q_{g,0}+Q_{g,1}$ are the same for every $g$. The table actions in Equations <a href=\"#eq:table-flip-action\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:table-flip-action\">[eq:table-flip-action]</a> and <a href=\"#eq:table-translation-action\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:table-translation-action\">[eq:table-translation-action]</a> hold for each shift coefficient at this fixed context, with $q_v=Q_{1,0}+Q_{1,1}$ and $q_{v,g1}=Q_{g,1}$.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-hashing",
        "exact_quote": "Let $H$ be a finite-dimensional vector space over $\\mathbb F_2$, write $\\chi_q(u)=(-1)^{q(u)}$ for $q\\in H^*$ and $u\\in H$, let $\\mathcal V$ be a real Hilbert space, let $s\\ge1$ be an integer, and let $$c(q,z)\\in\\mathcal V,\n \\qquad q\\in H^*,\\quad z\\in\\mathbb F_2^s.$$ Set $$w_q=\\sum_{z\\in\\mathbb F_2^s}\\norm{c(q,z)}^2,\n \\qquad M=\\sum_{q\\in H^*}w_q.$$ Choose $L,J_1,\\ldots,J_s$ independently and uniformly in $H$, and define $$\\mathbf J(q)=(q(J_1),\\ldots,q(J_s)),\n \\qquad\n U(z)=\\sum_{q\\in H^*}\\chi_q(L)c(q,z+\\mathbf J(q)).$$ Then $$\\label{eq:hashing-total-mass}\n \\mathbb E\\sum_{z\\in\\mathbb F_2^s}\\norm{U(z)}^2=M.$$ For every $\\zeta>0$, there are $\\gamma>0$ and $s_0\\in\\mathbb N$, depending only on $\\zeta$, such that for every $s\\ge s_0$, $$\\label{eq:hashing-dispersion}\n \\max_{q\\in H^*}w_q\\le\\gamma M\n \\quad\\Longrightarrow\\quad\n \\mathbb E\\max_{z\\in\\mathbb F_2^s}\\norm{U(z)}^2\\le\\zeta M.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-102.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-hashing",
          "exact_quote": "Let $H$ be a finite-dimensional vector space over $\\mathbb F_2$, write $\\chi_q(u)=(-1)^{q(u)}$ for $q\\in H^*$ and $u\\in H$, let $\\mathcal V$ be a real Hilbert space, let $s\\ge1$ be an integer, and let $$c(q,z)\\in\\mathcal V,\n \\qquad q\\in H^*,\\quad z\\in\\mathbb F_2^s.$$ Set $$w_q=\\sum_{z\\in\\mathbb F_2^s}\\norm{c(q,z)}^2,\n \\qquad M=\\sum_{q\\in H^*}w_q.$$ Choose $L,J_1,\\ldots,J_s$ independently and uniformly in $H$, and define $$\\mathbf J(q)=(q(J_1),\\ldots,q(J_s)),\n \\qquad\n U(z)=\\sum_{q\\in H^*}\\chi_q(L)c(q,z+\\mathbf J(q)).$$ Then $$\\label{eq:hashing-total-mass}\n \\mathbb E\\sum_{z\\in\\mathbb F_2^s}\\norm{U(z)}^2=M.$$ For every $\\zeta>0$, there are $\\gamma>0$ and $s_0\\in\\mathbb N$, depending only on $\\zeta$, such that for every $s\\ge s_0$, $$\\label{eq:hashing-dispersion}\n \\max_{q\\in H^*}w_q\\le\\gamma M\n \\quad\\Longrightarrow\\quad\n \\mathbb E\\max_{z\\in\\mathbb F_2^s}\\norm{U(z)}^2\\le\\zeta M.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-102.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $H$ be a finite-dimensional vector space over $\\mathbb F_2$, write $\\chi_q(u)=(-1)^{q(u)}$ for $q\\in H^*$ and $u\\in H$, let $\\mathcal V$ be a real Hilbert space, let $s\\ge1$ be an integer, and let $$c(q,z)\\in\\mathcal V,\n \\qquad q\\in H^*,\\quad z\\in\\mathbb F_2^s.$$ Set $$w_q=\\sum_{z\\in\\mathbb F_2^s}\\norm{c(q,z)}^2,\n \\qquad M=\\sum_{q\\in H^*}w_q.$$ Choose $L,J_1,\\ldots,J_s$ independently and uniformly in $H$, and define $$\\mathbf J(q)=(q(J_1),\\ldots,q(J_s)),\n \\qquad\n U(z)=\\sum_{q\\in H^*}\\chi_q(L)c(q,z+\\mathbf J(q)).$$ Then $$\\label{eq:hashing-total-mass}\n \\mathbb E\\sum_{z\\in\\mathbb F_2^s}\\norm{U(z)}^2=M.$$ For every $\\zeta>0$, there are $\\gamma>0$ and $s_0\\in\\mathbb N$, depending only on $\\zeta$, such that for every $s\\ge s_0$, $$\\label{eq:hashing-dispersion}\n \\max_{q\\in H^*}w_q\\le\\gamma M\n \\quad\\Longrightarrow\\quad\n \\mathbb E\\max_{z\\in\\mathbb F_2^s}\\norm{U(z)}^2\\le\\zeta M.$$",
      "explanation": "Character orthogonality preserves total mass, while diffuse character weights bound the expected largest hashed vector.",
      "fidelity": "PENDING",
      "hypotheses": [

          "exact_quote": "Let $H$ be a finite-dimensional vector space over $\\mathbb F_2$, write $\\chi_q(u)=(-1)^{q(u)}$ for $q\\in H^*$ and $u\\in H$, let $\\mathcal V$ be a real Hilbert space, let $s\\ge1$ be an integer, and let $$c(q,z)\\in\\mathcal V,\n \\qquad q\\in H^*,\\quad z\\in\\mathbb F_2^s.$$ Set $$w_q=\\sum_{z\\in\\mathbb F_2^s}\\norm{c(q,z)}^2,\n \\qquad M=\\sum_{q\\in H^*}w_q.$$ Choose $L,J_1,\\ldots,J_s$ independently and uniformly in $H$, and define $$\\mathbf J(q)=(q(J_1),\\ldots,q(J_s)),\n \\qquad\n U(z)=\\sum_{q\\in H^*}\\chi_q(L)c(q,z+\\mathbf J(q)).$$ Then $$\\label{eq:hashing-total-mass}\n \\mathbb E\\sum_{z\\in\\mathbb F_2^s}\\norm{U(z)}^2=M.$$ For every $\\zeta>0$, there are $\\gamma>0$ and $s_0\\in\\mathbb N$, depending only on $\\zeta$, such that for every $s\\ge s_0$, $$\\label{eq:hashing-dispersion}\n \\max_{q\\in H^*}w_q\\le\\gamma M\n \\quad\\Longrightarrow\\quad\n \\mathbb E\\max_{z\\in\\mathbb F_2^s}\\norm{U(z)}^2\\le\\zeta M.$$",

        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-hashing",
          "exact_quote": "Let $H$ be a finite-dimensional vector space over $\\mathbb F_2$, write $\\chi_q(u)=(-1)^{q(u)}$ for $q\\in H^*$ and $u\\in H$, let $\\mathcal V$ be a real Hilbert space, let $s\\ge1$ be an integer, and let $$c(q,z)\\in\\mathcal V,\n \\qquad q\\in H^*,\\quad z\\in\\mathbb F_2^s.$$ Set $$w_q=\\sum_{z\\in\\mathbb F_2^s}\\norm{c(q,z)}^2,\n \\qquad M=\\sum_{q\\in H^*}w_q.$$ Choose $L,J_1,\\ldots,J_s$ independently and uniformly in $H$, and define $$\\mathbf J(q)=(q(J_1),\\ldots,q(J_s)),\n \\qquad\n U(z)=\\sum_{q\\in H^*}\\chi_q(L)c(q,z+\\mathbf J(q)).$$ Then $$\\label{eq:hashing-total-mass}\n \\mathbb E\\sum_{z\\in\\mathbb F_2^s}\\norm{U(z)}^2=M.$$ For every $\\zeta>0$, there are $\\gamma>0$ and $s_0\\in\\mathbb N$, depending only on $\\zeta$, such that for every $s\\ge s_0$, $$\\label{eq:hashing-dispersion}\n \\max_{q\\in H^*}w_q\\le\\gamma M\n \\quad\\Longrightarrow\\quad\n \\mathbb E\\max_{z\\in\\mathbb F_2^s}\\norm{U(z)}^2\\le\\zeta M.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-102.2"
        }
      ],
      "statement_anchor": "stmt-lem-hashing"
    },
    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-extraction",
        "exact_quote": "Fix $t\\in(0,1)$, $0<\\delta<1-B(t)$, and $0<\\mu<1/2$. There are tree parameters $s\\ge2,m\\ge1$ and constants $p_*,\\theta>0$, depending only on $t,\\delta,\\mu$, with the following property. For any affine label space $\\mathcal A$ and any family $\\phi_\\omega$ as above, if $$\\label{eq:excess-stability}\n \\mathbb E_{\\omega,\\mathcal D,R,F_{\\mathrm{out}}}\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{Stab}}_t^{F_{\\mathcal D}}(\\bar f_\\omega)\n \\ge B(t)+\\delta,$$ then $$\\label{eq:extraction-event}\n \\Pr\\left[\n   \\max_{q\\in\\mathcal A}\n   m_q(\\bar f_\\omega;\\Gamma,F_{\\mathrm{out}})\\ge\\theta\n \\right]\\ge p_*.$$ Here we sample $\\omega$, the random slice $\\mathcal D$, and a uniform node $v\\in\\mathcal D$; conditional on these, we sample $F_{\\mathrm{out}}$ and $\\Gamma$ independently and uniformly.",
        "source_id": "PM-102.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-unsmooth",
        "exact_quote": "For every fixed $\\underline y$, $\\mathcal D$, $v$, $\\Gamma$, $F_{\\mathrm{out}}$, and label $q\\in\\mathcal A$, $$\\label{eq:unsmooth}\n m_q(\\bar G_{\\underline y};\\Gamma,F_{\\mathrm{out}})\n \\leq\n \\mathbb E_{E,\\,\\underline e\\mid\\underline y}\n m_q(G_{\\underline e};\\Gamma+E_\\Gamma,F_{\\mathrm{out}}).$$ Here the noise $E$ is independent of the conditionally sampled edge tuple $\\underline e$.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-planting",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\mathsf U$ be the unplanted law and $\\mathsf P$ the planted law after forgetting the mark. Both laws are taken on the full data $$\\bigl(\\underline e,J_0,\\mathcal D,v,F_{\\mathrm{out}},\n        \\Gamma,R^{\\mathrm{new}},B\\bigr).$$ Then $$\\label{eq:planting-tv}\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{TV}}(\\mathsf P,\\mathsf U)\n \\leq \\frac12\\sqrt{\\frac{1-p_0}{Tp_0}}\n \\leq \\frac{1}{2\\sqrt{Tp_0}}.$$ Consequently, if $$\\label{eq:decoder-repetition}\n T\\geq\\frac{1}{p_0(p_*\\theta)^2},$$ then under the planted law, $$\\label{eq:planted-score}\n \\mathbb E_{\\mathsf P}\\,m_{q^*}(G_{\\underline e};\n               \\Gamma+E_\\Gamma,F_{\\mathrm{out}})\n \\geq \\frac{p_*\\theta}{2}.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-102.2"
      },
      "evidence": [

        "exact_quote": "Fix a context and the outside tables. Let $\\widetilde f(\\widetilde L,F_{\\mathcal D})$ be a function on $\\widetilde H^{2s}$ and the slice tables. For every label $q\\in\\mathcal A$, $$\\label{eq:restriction-mass}\n m_q(f)\\leq 2^{2s}\n       \\sum_{\\substack{\\widetilde q\\in\\widetilde{\\mathcal A}\\\\\n                       \\rho(\\widetilde q)=q}}\n          \\widetilde m_{\\widetilde q}(\\widetilde f).$$ The masses on both sides use the common-pair-sum definition of Equation <a href=\"#eq:mass\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:mass\">[eq:mass]</a>, with the respective affine row spaces.",

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-affine-validity-invariance",
        "exact_quote": "Let $X$ have dimension $3k_0$, let $H\\le X$ have dimension $k_0$, and let $h:H\\to\\mathbb F_2$ be linear. Suppose a table $F$ assigns a linear function to every $\\ell$-space, and that for every $(\\ell+k_0)$-space $K\\supseteq H$ there is a linear function $f_K:K\\to\\mathbb F_2$ such that $f_K|_H=h$ and $F[L]=f_K|_L$ whenever $L+H=K$. Fix a $q$-space $Q$ with $Q\\cap H=\\{0\\}$, and set $$e_{k_0}=2^{\\ell+r-2k_0+1}.$$ Assume $q\\le\\ell$, $3k_0-r>\\ell$, <a href=\"#eq:affine-validity-ell-margin\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:affine-validity-ell-margin\">[eq:affine-validity-ell-margin]</a>, and $e_{k_0}\\le\\tau_0/4$. For $Q\\subseteq W\\subseteq X$ of codimension $i\\le r$, say that $(g,W)$ occurs if $g:W\\to\\mathbb F_2$ is linear and $$\\Pr_{Q\\subseteq L\\subseteq W,\\ \\dim L=\\ell}\n       [F[L]=g|_L]>\\tau_i.$$ Call it maximal if it has no occurring extension to a strictly larger space. Every maximal occurring pair satisfies $H\\subseteq W$ and $g|_H=h$.",
        "source_id": "PM-102.2"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-unweighted",
        "exact_quote": "Let $G$ be a graph on $N$ vertices with nonnegative rational weights $w_{uv}$ on unordered distinct vertex pairs, and $\\sum_{u<v}w_{uv}\\le1$. For every fixed $\\epsilon'>0$, one can construct in deterministic polynomial time a simple unweighted graph $G'$ and an integer scale $Q$ such that $$\\left|\\frac{\\operatorname{MaxCut}(G')}{Q}\n             -\\operatorname{MaxCut}(G)\\right|\\le\\epsilon'.$$ The same conclusion holds when the input graph has loops, after deleting them without renormalizing its other weights.",
        "source_id": "PM-102.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-unweighted",
          "exact_quote": "Let $G$ be a graph on $N$ vertices with nonnegative rational weights $w_{uv}$ on unordered distinct vertex pairs, and $\\sum_{u<v}w_{uv}\\le1$.           "source_id": "PM-102.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $G$ be a graph on $N$ vertices with nonnegative rational weights $w_{uv}$ on unordered distinct vertex pairs, and $\\sum_{u<v}w_{uv}\\le1$.       "explanation": "A deterministic cluster construction preserves maximum cut on a common scale with fixed additive error.",

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Max-Cut** ([source](#eq:gw-constant)): For a graph with nonnegative edge weights, Max-Cut asks for a partition of the vertices maximizing the weight of edges crossing the partition.
- **Approximation factor** ([source](#eq:gw-constant)): For a maximization problem, an $\alpha$-approximation returns a feasible solution worth at least $\alpha$ times the optimum.
- **Affine row space** ([source](#eq:labels-dual)): Let $\mathcal A$ be a nonempty affine space over $\mathbb F_2$, with a fixed affine coordinate system, and set $$H=\mathop{\mathrm{Aff}}(\mathcal A,\mathbb F_2).$$ This is a vector space, containing the constant form $\mathbf 1$.
- **Row-noise operator** ([source](#pm-h-6)): For $0\le r\le1$, let $T_r$ be the row-noise operator: independently in each coordinate, retain the row with probability $r$ and otherwise replace it by an independent uniform row.
- **Tree code** ([source](#stmt-def-tree-code)): Fix integers $s\ge2$ and $m\ge1$, and put $$k=(2s)^m,\qquad d=s^m.$$ Let $\mathcal T$ be the complete rooted $2s$-ary tree of depth $m$. The children of each internal node $v$ are indexed by $(g,b)\in[s]\times\{0,1\}$. Its leaves are indexed by $[k]$. To each internal node assign a table $F_v:\mathbb F_2^s\to\{-1,1\}$, and to each leaf assign a row $R_i\in H$. The word $W(R,F)\in\{-1,1\}^{\mathcal A}$ is defined pointwise as follows. At $a\in\mathcal A$, leaf $i$ has sign $(-1)^{R_i(a)}$. An internal node with child signs $(Z_{g,b})$ outputs $$\left(\prod_{g=1}^s Z_{g,0}\right)F_v(z), \qquad (-1)^{z_g}=Z_{g,0}Z_{g,1}. \label{eq:gate-rule}$$ The root output is $W(R,F)(a)$.
- **Function of the code** ([source](#eq:function-of-code)): We call $f$ a *function of the code* if $$f(R,F)=\phi(W(R,F)) \label{eq:function-of-code}$$ for a fixed function $\phi$ on words.
- **Unsplit Fourier index** ([source](#pm-h-15)): Call $C$ *unsplit at $v$* if, for every $g$, the ordered pair $(q_{v,g0},q_{v,g1})$ is either $(q_v,0)$ or $(0,q_v)$.
- **Label mass** ([source](#eq:mass)): For a label $q\in\mathcal A$ (equivalently, $q\in H^*$ with $q(\mathbf 1)=1$), define $$\label{eq:mass} m_q(f;\Gamma,F_{\mathrm{out}}) =\mathbb E_{F_{\mathcal D}} \sum_{\substack{Q\in(H^*)^{2s}\\ Q_{g0}+Q_{g1}=q\ (g\in[s])}} \abs{\widehat f^{\,\mathrm{shifts}}(Q)}^2.$$ The dependence on $v,\mathcal D$, and $\omega$ is suppressed.

| id | quantity | relation | value | regime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Q-GW | \alpha_{\mathrm{GW}} | = | $0.878567\ldots$ | The minimizing correlation is unrestricted in the displayed interval. |
| Q-B | B(t) | = | $\frac{2}{\pi}\arcsin t$ | A fixed correlation parameter. |
| Q-k | k | = | $(2s)^m$ | Tree parameters are fixed before the source game is chosen. |
| Q-d | d | = | $s^m$ | Tree parameters are fixed before the source game is chosen. |
| Q-Bernoulli | r^{2d} | ≥ | $1-2\mu$ | Positive integer monomial degree. |
| Q-completeness | cut value | ≥ | $\frac{1+t}{2}-t\mu-2Ta$ | The useful prefactor regime is stated in the source. |
| Q-unsplit | averaged unsplit projection loss | ≤ | $\frac{s}{2e m\mu}$ | Bounded function and positive smoothing parameter as stated. |
| Q-p0 | p_0 | = | $2^{-n(2k-2s)}$ | Alphabet and tree dimensions are fixed before repetition. |
| Q-TV | total variation distance | ≤ | $\frac{1}{2\sqrt{Tp_0}}$ | Positive repetition and zero-slot probability. |
| Q-decoding | source game value | ≥ | $2^{-2s}\frac{p_*\theta}{2}$ | Extraction constants fixed before alphabet dimension. |
| Q-scale | Q | = | $p^{12}$ | Fixed additive accuracy. |
| Q-rectangle | scaled rectangle discrepancy | ≤ | $\frac1p+\frac1{p^2}$ | Nonnegative rational weights with total at most one. |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **stmt-lem-mis** [claim](#stmt-lem-mis) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-mis): Smooth the Boolean function and control high-degree influence. -> Degree-cutoff stability implication.
- **stmt-prop-affine-games** [claim](#stmt-prop-affine-games) / [proof](#sec:affine-games): Fold linear answer spaces, condition valid samples, complete tables and decode invariant lists. -> Affine projection game with soundness-fixed alphabet.
- **stmt-lem-tree-correlation** [claim](#stmt-lem-tree-correlation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-tree-correlation): Expand gate Walsh coefficients and intersect complementary traversals. -> Homogeneous word correlation.
- **stmt-lem-completeness** [claim](#stmt-lem-completeness) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-completeness): Use dictator labels and a union bound on sampled constraints. -> Completeness cut lower bound.
- **stmt-lem-cut-stability** [claim](#stmt-lem-cut-stability) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-cut-stability): Separate odd and even Fourier parity in the noised slice. -> Cut value controlled by odd stability.
- **stmt-lem-row-symmetry** [claim](#stmt-lem-row-symmetry) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-row-symmetry): Compare translations that preserve the generated word. -> Common child-pair sums and table actions.
- **stmt-lem-unsplit** [claim](#stmt-lem-unsplit) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-unsplit): Count split support and telescope across random depth. -> Average projection loss.
- **stmt-lem-shift-coordinates** [claim](#stmt-lem-shift-coordinates) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-shift-coordinates): Invert representative-row differences and regroup Fourier characters. -> Free-shift fiber description.
- **stmt-lem-hashing** [claim](#stmt-lem-hashing) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-hashing): Use character orthogonality, truncation and a fourth moment. -> Dimension-independent dispersion estimate.
- **stmt-prop-extraction** [claim](#stmt-prop-extraction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-extraction): Find an influential entry, discard even dependence and force heavy label mass by hashing. -> Positive probability of conditional heavy label.
- **stmt-lem-unsmooth** [claim](#stmt-lem-unsmooth) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-unsmooth): Apply convexity to a shift Fourier projection. -> Expected raw mass bounds smoothed mass.
- **stmt-lem-planting** [claim](#stmt-lem-planting) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-planting): Compute the density of forgotten planting and bound binomial fluctuations. -> Planted score retained after repetition.
- **stmt-lem-restriction** [claim](#stmt-lem-restriction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-restriction): Sum lifted Fourier coefficients and use Cauchy–Schwarz. -> Affine restriction mass bound.
- **stmt-prop-decoding** [claim](#stmt-prop-decoding) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-decoding): Construct endpoint strategies with public unmarked edges and zero marked coefficients. -> Positive source-game value.
- **stmt-lem-unweighted** [claim](#stmt-lem-unweighted) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-unweighted): Use finite-field cluster adjacency and rectangle discrepancy. -> Simple unweighted graph preserving scaled maximum cut.
- **stmt-thm-gap** [claim](#stmt-thm-gap) / [proof](#sec:completion): Choose soundness before alphabet, repetition and completeness; enumerate rational test outcomes. -> NP-hard rational weighted cut gap.
- **stmt-thm-main** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#sec:completion): Choose a rational gap below the approximation factor and convert to unweighted graphs. -> Hardness for simple unweighted graphs.

For any affine label space $\\mathcal A$ and any family $\\phi_\\omega$ as above, if $$\\label{eq:excess-stability}\n \\mathbb E_{\\omega,\\mathcal D,R,F_{\\mathrm{out}}}\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{Stab}}_t^{F_{\\mathcal D}}(\\bar f_\\omega)\n \\ge B(t)+\\delta,$$ then $$\\label{eq:extraction-event}\n \\Pr\\left[\n   \\max_{q\\in\\mathcal A}\n   m_q(\\bar f_\\omega;\\Gamma,F_{\\mathrm{out}})\\ge\\theta\n \\right]\\ge p_*.$$ Here we sample $\\omega$, the random slice $\\mathcal D$, and a uniform node $v\\in\\mathcal D$; conditional on these, we sample $F_{\\mathrm{out}}$ and $\\Gamma$ independently and uniformly.",

          "exact_quote": "Fix a context and the outside tables. Let $\\widetilde f(\\widetilde L,F_{\\mathcal D})$ be a function on $\\widetilde H^{2s}$ and the slice tables. Suppose its shift Fourier support has the common-pair-sum property $$\\widetilde Q_{g0}+\\widetilde Q_{g1}\n =\\widetilde Q_{h0}+\\widetilde Q_{h1}\n \\quad\\text{for all }g,h\\in[s].$$ Set $f(L,F_{\\mathcal D})=\n\\widetilde f(\\iota L,F_{\\mathcal D})$, where $\\iota$ acts on each shift. For every label $q\\in\\mathcal A$, $$\\label{eq:restriction-mass}\n m_q(f)\\leq 2^{2s}\n       \\sum_{\\substack{\\widetilde q\\in\\widetilde{\\mathcal A}\\\\\n                       \\rho(\\widetilde q)=q}}\n          \\widetilde m_{\\widetilde q}(\\widetilde f).$$ The masses on both sides use the common-pair-sum definition of Equation <a href=\"#eq:mass\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:mass\">[eq:mass]</a>, with the respective affine row spaces.",

        }

For every fixed $\\epsilon'>0$, one can construct in deterministic polynomial time a simple unweighted graph $G'$ and an integer scale $Q$ such that $$\\left|\\frac{\\operatorname{MaxCut}(G')}{Q}\n             -\\operatorname{MaxCut}(G)\\right|\\le\\epsilon'.$$ The same conclusion holds when the input graph has loops, after deleting them without renormalizing its other weights.",

For graphs equipped with a rational probability distribution on edges, it is NP-hard to distinguish $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\textnormal{YES:}\\quad&\\mathop{\\mathrm{Val}}\\ge\\frac{1+t}{2}-\\varepsilon,\\\\\n \\textnormal{NO:}\\quad&\\mathop{\\mathrm{Val}}\\le\\frac{1+B(t)}{2}+\\varepsilon,\n\\end{aligned}$$ where $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Val}}$ is the maximum cut weight.",

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **gate-expansion**: The Walsh expansion of a gate keeps one child from every pair; its multilinear monomials have the prescribed degree.
- **word-information**: A cut function receives only the resulting word, keeping generator data hidden.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **hardness-as-code**: Treat finite gate tests as certification of unconditional approximation hardness. -> The reduction and its external analytic hypotheses remain outside the finite gate check.
- **hidden-inputs**: Let a cut function inspect the generating rows or sampled edges. -> The information boundary required by extraction no longer holds.
- **weight-domain**: Apply weighted-to-unweighted conversion to negative or arbitrary real weights. -> The stated conversion guarantee does not cover that weight domain.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `constructive`. The gate rule is a finite constructive map from child signs and a Boolean table to a root sign. The code checks the local Walsh expansion; it does not certify the hardness reduction or arbitrary tree depths.

```python
# INPUTS: JSON object s and tables (integer truth-table encodings).
# PRECONDITIONS: s in {2,3}; nonempty tables list of at most 256 encodings in [0,2**(2**s)).
# OUTPUTS: SHA256 of ordered gate truth values, table-major then child-mask order.
# TIME: O(len(tables)*s*2**(2*s)); bounded admitted inputs only.
# SPACE: O(s); incremental digest, excluding input storage.
# ARITHMETIC: exact python-flint fmpz signs and integer bit indexing.
# FAILURE: invalid inputs raise ValueError; no approximation or solver witness.
import hashlib, json, sys
from flint import fmpz
data = json.loads(sys.stdin.read())
s = data['s']
tables = data['tables']
if type(s) is not int or s not in (2, 3) or not isinstance(tables, list) or not 1 <= len(tables) <= 256:
    raise ValueError('domain')
if any(type(t) is not int or not 0 <= t < (1 << (1 << s)) for t in tables):
    raise ValueError('table')
digest = hashlib.sha256()
for table in tables:
    for mask in range(1 << (2*s)):
        signs = [fmpz(-1 if mask & (1 << j) else 1) for j in range(2*s)]
        z = 0
        prefactor = fmpz(1)
        for g in range(s):
            prefactor *= signs[2*g]
            if signs[2*g] != signs[2*g+1]:
                z |= 1 << g
        value = prefactor * (-1 if table & (1 << z) else 1)
        digest.update(b'1' if value == 1 else b'0')
print(digest.hexdigest())
```

| software | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | A graph-cut software component could adapt the deterministic weighted-to-unweighted conversion when its input satisfies the stated rational-weight and fixed-error conditions. No measured implementation is supplied. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The projection games are promise-problem inputs to a hardness reduction. A contract for gameplay, simulation fidelity, or interactive performance is ABSENT. |
| tools | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | A graph-instance preparation tool could adapt the conversion to produce simple unweighted Max-Cut instances on a common scale. Applicability requires the exact weighted input conditions. |
| algorithms | True | strong | adaptable | stated | The paper states a worst-case Max-Cut approximation barrier and supplies a deterministic conversion to simple unweighted graphs. The conversion preserves maximum cut on a common scale within a fixed additive error. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The finite graphs, rows, and tables support the proof construction. A data-structure interface or operation-cost guarantee is ABSENT. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The construction uses classical finite enumeration and linear algebra. An accelerator architecture, arithmetic throughput, or hardware resource contract is ABSENT. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The result concerns worst-case approximation hardness for Max-Cut. A cryptographic primitive, average-case assumption, or security reduction is ABSENT. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The noise stability and influential-coordinate arguments apply to bounded odd functions of the generated codeword in the stated reduction. A learning objective or statistical generalization contract is ABSENT. |
| optimisation_operations_research | True | strong | inspirational | inferred | The main theorem directly constrains universal polynomial-time approximation guarantees for Max-Cut on simple unweighted graphs. It is a worst-case barrier rather than a performance estimate for a particular workload. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The endpoint labelers are mathematical strategies with shared randomness and fixed background game and cut data. A replication, consensus, or fault-tolerance contract is ABSENT. |
| other (computational_complexity) | True | strong | inspirational | stated | The theorem establishes unconditional approximation hardness for Max-Cut; the affine-game input and dimension-independent decoding supply the reduction framework. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **fixed_ratio_scope** (hypothesis): The hardness claim concerns a fixed approximation factor strictly above the Goemans–Williamson constant on simple unweighted Max-Cut instances. Blocked use: ['Claiming hardness at the threshold itself from this theorem.', 'Transferring the claim to an objective other than Max-Cut.']
- **complexity_consequence** (hypothesis): The algorithmic consequence is conditional on the separation of polynomial-time computation from NP; the theorem supplies a worst-case barrier rather than a workload measurement. Blocked use: ['Interpreting hardness as a measured runtime or an unconditional impossibility of all algorithms.']
- **rounding_expectation** (approximation): The cited finite-precision optimization and rounding benchmark is an expected approximation guarantee with a fixed loss. Blocked use: ['Claiming a deterministic per-run guarantee from the stated expected rounding guarantee.']
- **relaxation_gap** (counterexample_scope): A limitation of the semidefinite relaxation alone does not establish hardness for arbitrary algorithms. Blocked use: ['Using an integrality-gap construction alone as an arbitrary-algorithm hardness proof.']
- **word_information_boundary** (hypothesis): The cut functions depend on resulting words and cannot access the hidden generating data. Blocked use: ['Applying the extraction conclusion to an analyzer that receives generating rows, tables, slices, or sampled edges.']
- **unsplit_locality** (hypothesis): The unsplit simplification is imposed locally at a gate; it is not a global simultaneous unsplit guarantee. Blocked use: ['Assuming the Fourier index is unsplit at every gate.']
- **projected_range** (edge_case): The projected function need not retain the pointwise range bound of the original function. Blocked use: ['Using a pointwise range bound for the projected function without a separate proof.']
- **decoder_efficiency** (nonconstructive): The endpoint strategies are used to lower-bound game value without an efficiency claim for the labelers. Blocked use: ['Publishing the endpoint decoding argument as an efficient implementation guarantee.']
- **parameter_order** (hypothesis): Soundness is fixed before the alphabet dimension, repetition, and completeness error; reversing that order risks circular parameter choices. Blocked use: ['Treating the alphabet dimension as independent of requested source soundness.']
- **fixed_parameter_size** (hypothesis): The polynomial-size graph claim fixes the target gap parameters; it does not provide a practical resource bound when those parameters vary. Blocked use: ['Reading polynomial size for fixed parameters as a uniform practical graph-size guarantee.']
- **rational_weight_conversion** (hypothesis): The unweighted conversion assumes nonnegative rational weights on distinct unordered pairs with total weight at most one and a fixed positive additive error. Blocked use: ['Applying the conversion to negative weights or arbitrary real weights.', 'Claiming a multiplicative preservation guarantee from the additive-error statement.']
- **loop_scale** (edge_case): Loops are deleted while retaining the original weight scale. Blocked use: ['Renormalizing the remaining edge weights when using the common-scale comparison.']
- **cluster_splitting** (hypothesis): The conversion must control arbitrary cuts that split clusters, not merely cuts constant within clusters. Blocked use: ['Checking only cluster-constant cuts as a certificate for the conversion.']
- **formal_certificate_scope** (formal_scope): An executable check of a finite gate identity does not by itself certify the full hardness reduction; a formal certificate of the complete argument is ABSENT in the supplied paper. Blocked use: ['Treating finite gate enumeration as independent verification of the full hardness theorem.']

### Formal statement (Lean 4)

Corpus status: formalized in the source corpus (toolchain `leanprover/lean4:v4.34.1`, Mathlib `d13f23b723b8a846827a245b89c10fc7d3f11612`). Not re-checked here; the statements give the exact quantifiers and hypotheses, not a proof.

Scope note of family 102 (`lean/docs/102.md`, verbatim):

> The following describes the scope of the Lean formalization related to the following accompanying paper(s):
>
> - [The Unique Games Theorem](../../preprints/The-Unique-Games-Theorem-September-23-2026/paper.pdf)
> - [A Direct Proof of Optimal Max-Cut Hardness](../../preprints/A-Direct-Proof-of-Optimal-Max-Cut-Hardness-September-23-2026/paper.pdf)
> - [The Factor-Two Hardness Threshold for Vertex Cover](../../preprints/The-Factor-Two-Hardness-Threshold-for-Vertex-Cover-September-23-2026/paper.pdf)
> - [Constant-factor hardness of Min-UnCut](../../preprints/Constant-factor-hardness-of-Min-UnCut-September-23-2026/paper.pdf)
> - [Constant-factor hardness of directed feedback vertex set](../../preprints/Constant-factor-hardness-of-directed-feedback-vertex-set-September-23-2026/paper.pdf)
>
> ## Scope
>
> The Unique Games conjecture asks for hardness of distinguishing nearly satisfiable unique games from games of very small value. The formalized result gives, for every fixed $0<\varepsilon,\delta<1/2$, a deterministic polynomial-time reduction from binary 3SAT to nonempty unweighted simple bipartite unique games. Satisfiable inputs have value at least $1-\varepsilon$ and unsatisfiable inputs have value at most $\delta$. The alphabet is a fixed $\mathbb F_2^s$ depending only on the errors, and every constraint is a translation.
>
> The formalized result establishes hardness of approximating Max-Cut beyond the Goemans–Williamson constant $\alpha_{\mathrm{GW}}$. For every fixed $\alpha_{\mathrm{GW}}<\alpha\le1$, it gives a deterministic polynomial-time reduction from binary 3SAT to a strictly separated Max-Cut gap on finite simple unweighted graphs. The reduction includes the explicit positive integer scaling used in the gap statement.
>
> The formalized result gives the factor-two hardness threshold for Vertex Cover. For each integer $m\ge4$, a polynomial-time reduction from binary 3SAT produces simple unweighted graphs with cover density below $1/2+1/m$ on satisfiable inputs and above $1-1/m$ on unsatisfiable inputs. Consequently, any polynomial-time approximation with a fixed factor $1\le\alpha<2$ would give a polynomial-time decision algorithm for 3SAT. No assumption that $P\ne NP$ is built into the statement.
>
> Min-UnCut minimizes the number of edges left uncut by a bipartition. The formalization constructs a deterministic reduction from encoded $3$-SAT formulas to finite simple unweighted Min-UnCut instances with arbitrarily large fixed multiplicative gaps. For every integer $K\ge2$, satisfiable formulas give optimum at most the output threshold, while unsatisfiable formulas give optimum strictly greater than $K$ times that threshold. Runtime and output length are polynomial for each fixed $K$, establishing hardness for every fixed approximation factor greater than one.
>
> A directed feedback vertex set meets every directed cycle. The formalization proves hardness of approximating the minimum such set within any fixed constant factor. For every real factor $A\ge1$ and every language in NP, it constructs a polynomial-time gap reduction to unweighted directed graphs with the stated completeness and soundness separation. Thus a fixed-factor polynomial-time approximation would imply a polynomial-time algorithm for every NP language.

Comparator source `OptimalMaxCut.lean` (5514 bytes, sha256 `a30ac1bd42af8f3602bbfb73c689c755fd2d8c74cae8fd58b63a8ed42c5e6b0d`), verbatim:

```lean
import Mathlib

namespace OAI

namespace MaxCutGames.BinaryFormula

structure Literal where
  name : Nat
  positive : Bool
  deriving DecidableEq

def Literal.eval (literal : Literal) (assignment : Nat → Bool) : Bool :=
  if literal.positive then assignment literal.name else !(assignment literal.name)

abbrev Clause := Vector Literal 3

def Clause.eval (clause : Clause) (assignment : Nat → Bool) : Bool :=
  (clause[0].eval assignment || clause[1].eval assignment) || clause[2].eval assignment

/-- An ordinary conjunction of three-slot clauses with sparse variable names.
Repeated names and repeated literals are permitted in every clause. -/
structure Formula where
  clauses : List Clause

def Formula.Satisfiable (F : Formula) : Prop :=
  ∃ assignment : Nat → Bool, ∀ clause ∈ F.clauses, clause.eval assignment = true

end MaxCutGames.BinaryFormula

namespace MaxCutGames.BinaryEncoding

open BinaryFormula

def bitsValue : List Bool → Nat
  | [] => 0
  | b :: bits => Nat.bit b (bitsValue bits)

/-- Prefix-free framing on the two-symbol input alphabet. -/
def frame : List Bool → List Bool
  | [] => [false]
  | b :: bits => true :: b :: frame bits

def parseFrame : List Bool → Option (List Bool × List Bool)
  | false :: rest => some ([], rest)
  | true :: b :: rest => do
      let (bits, trailing) ← parseFrame rest
      return (b :: bits, trailing)
  | _ => none

def nameBits (name : Nat) : List Bool := frame name.bits

/-- Equality with the canonical digits rejects alternate padded encodings. -/
def parseName (input : List Bool) : Option (Nat × List Bool) := do
  let (digits, rest) ← parseFrame input
  let name := bitsValue digits
  if digits = name.bits then some (name, rest) else none

def parseLiteral : List Bool → Option (Literal × List Bool)
  | sign :: input => do
      let (name, rest) ← parseName input
      return (⟨name, sign⟩, rest)
  | [] => none

def parseClause (input : List Bool) : Option (Clause × List Bool) := do
  let (a, input) ← parseLiteral input
  let (b, input) ← parseLiteral input
  let (c, rest) ← parseLiteral input
  return (#v[a, b, c], rest)

/-- Fuel bounds the number of clause iterations, not the size of any name. -/
def parseClauses : Nat → List Bool → Option (List Clause × List Bool)
  | 0, _ => none
  | _ + 1, false :: rest => some ([], rest)
  | fuel + 1, true :: input => do
      let (clause, input) ← parseClause input
      let (clauses, rest) ← parseClauses fuel input
      return (clause :: clauses, rest)
  | _ + 1, [] => none

def decodeFormula (input : List Bool) : Option Formula := do
  let (clauses, rest) ← parseClauses (input.length + 1) input
  if rest = [] then some ⟨clauses⟩ else none

end MaxCutGames.BinaryEncoding

namespace OptimalMaxCut

open scoped BigOperators

/-- The exact Goemans–Williamson ratio, as an infimum over -1 ≤ ρ < 1. -/
noncomputable def alphaGW : ℝ :=
  sInf ((fun ρ : ℝ => 2 * Real.arccos ρ / (Real.pi * (1 - ρ))) ''
    Set.Ico (-1 : ℝ) 1)

/-- The ordinary binary 3SAT language, including rejection of malformed words. -/
def binary3SAT (input : List Bool) : Prop :=
  ∃ formula, MaxCutGames.BinaryEncoding.decodeFormula input = some formula ∧
    formula.Satisfiable

/-- A finite simple unweighted graph with its full Boolean adjacency table. -/
structure Graph where
  vertices : ℕ
  adj : Fin vertices → Fin vertices → Bool
  symmetric : ∀ u v, adj u v = adj v u
  loopless : ∀ u, adj u u = false

/-- Each unordered distinct edge crossing the cut is counted exactly once. -/
def Graph.cutSize (G : Graph) (cut : Fin G.vertices → Bool) : ℕ :=
  ∑ u : Fin G.vertices, ∑ v : Fin G.vertices,
    if u < v ∧ G.adj u v = true ∧ cut u ≠ cut v then 1 else 0

/-- The maximum over all Boolean cuts of the finite vertex set. -/
def Graph.maxCut (G : Graph) : ℕ :=
  (Finset.univ.image G.cutSize).sup id

/-- The positive integer scale Q is part of the output. It need not equal the
number of graph edges. -/
structure ScaledGraph where
  graph : Graph
  scale : ℕ
  scale_pos : 0 < scale

/-- Canonical delimited binary vertex count and scale, followed by the full
adjacency table in lexicographic order. -/
def ScaledGraph.bits (G : ScaledGraph) : List Bool :=
  MaxCutGames.BinaryEncoding.nameBits G.graph.vertices ++
  MaxCutGames.BinaryEncoding.nameBits G.scale ++
  (List.finRange G.graph.vertices).flatMap (fun u =>
    (List.finRange G.graph.vertices).map (fun v => G.graph.adj u v))

/-- The rational gap bounds and the finite machine are fixed before the input.
The bounds compare the maximum cut divided by the positive output scale. -/
structure GapReduction (α : ℝ) where
  yesBound : ℚ
  noBound : ℚ
  yes_pos : 0 < yesBound
  no_nonneg : 0 ≤ noBound
  gap : (noBound : ℝ) < α * (yesBound : ℝ)
  construct : List Bool → ScaledGraph
  computation : Turing.TM2ComputableInPolyTime (id : List Bool → List Bool)
    ScaledGraph.bits construct
  finiteAlphabet : ∀ k, Finite (computation.tm.Γ k)
  completeness : ∀ input, binary3SAT input →
    (yesBound : ℝ) ≤ ((construct input).graph.maxCut : ℝ) / (construct input).scale
  soundness : ∀ input, ¬ binary3SAT input →
    ((construct input).graph.maxCut : ℝ) / (construct input).scale ≤ (noBound : ℝ)

def MainStatement : Prop :=
  ∀ α : ℝ, alphaGW < α → α ≤ 1 → Nonempty (GapReduction α)

end OptimalMaxCut

theorem OptimalMaxCut.main : OptimalMaxCut.MainStatement := by
  sorry

end OAI
```

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We prove that approximating Max-Cut on simple unweighted graphs within any fixed factor greater than the Goemans–Williamson constant is NP-hard.

<a id="pm-h-1"></a>
#### Statements index
